Q: Who's responsibility is case law?

Q: What was the significance of von Neumann's model of expanding economy?
A: "Von Neumann's model of an expanding economy is significant because it proved the existence and uniqueness of an equilibrium using his generalization of the Brouwer fixed-point theorem. This model raised the intellectual and mathematical level of economics by introducing concepts such as linear inequalities, complementary slackness, and saddlepoint duality. It has been viewed as a foundational contribution to mathematical economics, influencing the study of economic systems and computational economics. Von Neumann's work laid the groundwork for further developments in economic theory and the application of mathematical methods to economics."

Q: What could be a factor of lower energy consumption for electric trains?
A: "A factor of lower energy consumption for electric trains could be their higher efficiency compared to other forms of transportation. Electric trains typically have lower friction and can utilize regenerative braking, which allows them to recover energy during braking and feed it back into the system.
